import math
from collections import defaultdict
import heapq

def point_on_segment(p, start, end):
    x, y = p
    x1, y1 = start
    x2, y2 = end
    
    # Check if point is within bounds of the segment
    if (min(x1, x2) <= x <= max(x1, x2) and 
        min(y1, y2) <= y <= max(y1, y2)):
        
        # Check if the point is collinear with the segment
        cross_product = (x2 - x1) * (y - y1) - (y2 - y1) * (x - x1)
        if abs(cross_product) < 1e-9:
            return True
    return False

def line_intersection(seg1_start, seg1_end, seg2_start, seg2_end):
    x1, y1 = seg1_start
    x2, y2 = seg1_end
    x3, y3 = seg2_start
    x4, y4 = seg2_end
    
    denom = (x1 - x2) * (y3 - y4) - (y1 - y2) * (x3 - x4)
    if abs(denom) < 1e-10:
        return None
    
    t = ((x1 - x3) * (y3 - y4) - (y1 - y3) * (x3 - x4)) / denom
    u = ((x1 - x3) * (y1 - y2) - (y1 - y3) * (x1 - x2)) / denom
    
    if 0 <= t <= 1 and 0 <= u <= 1:
        x = x1 + t * (x2 - x1)
        y = y1 + t * (y2 - y1)
        return (x, y)
    
    return None

def distance(p1, p2):
    return math.sqrt((p1[0] - p2[0])**2 + (p1[1] - p2[1])**2)

def solve():
    while True:
        n = int(input().strip())
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        xs, ys = map(int, input().split())
        xg, yg = map(int, input().split())
        
        segments = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x1, y1, x2, y2 = map(int, input().split())
            segments.append(((x1, y1), (x2, y2)))
        
        # Find all intersection points (where at least two segments meet)
        intersection_points = set()
        
        # Find intersections between all pairs of segments
        for i in range(len(segments)):
            for j in range(i + 1, len(segments)):
                seg1 = segments[i]
                seg2 = segments[j]
                intersection = line_intersection(seg1[0], seg1[1], seg2[0], seg2[1])
                if intersection:
                    intersection_points.add(intersection)
        
        # Build graph - nodes are intersection points or start/end points
        # Edges represent valid travel between points
        graph = defaultdict(list)
        
        # Add edges between intersection points on the same street segment
        for seg_start, seg_end in segments:
            # Find all intersection points on this segment
            points_on_seg = []
            for pt in intersection_points:
                if point_on_segment(pt, seg_start, seg_end):
                    points_on_seg.append(pt)
            
            # Add edges between consecutive points on this segment
            for i in range(len(points_on_seg)):
                for j in range(i + 1, len(points_on_seg)):
                    dist = distance(points_on_seg[i], points_on_seg[j])
                    graph[points_on_seg[i]].append((points_on_seg[j], dist))
                    graph[points_on_seg[j]].append((points_on_seg[i], dist))
        
        # Connect start and goal points to their intersection neighbors
        # Start point connections
        start_connections = []
        for pt in intersection_points:
            for seg_start, seg_end in segments:
                if (xs, ys) == seg_start or (xs, ys) == seg_end:
                    if point_on_segment(pt, seg_start, seg_end):
                        start_connections.append(pt)
        
        for pt in start_connections:
            dist = distance((xs, ys), pt)
            graph[(xs, ys)].append((pt, dist))
            graph[pt].append(((xs, ys), dist))
        
        # Goal point connections
        goal_connections = []
        for pt in intersection_points:
            for seg_start, seg_end in segments:
                if (xg, yg) == seg_start or (xg, yg) == seg_end:
                    if point_on_segment(pt, seg_start, seg_end):
                        goal_connections.append(pt)
        
        for pt in goal_connections:
            dist = distance((xg, yg), pt)
            graph[(xg, yg)].append((pt, dist))
            graph[pt].append(((xg, yg), dist))
        
        # Run Dijkstra to find shortest path from start to goal
        start_node = (xs, ys)
        goal_node = (xg, yg)
        
        if start_node not in graph or goal_node not in graph:
            print(-1)
            continue
            
        # Dijkstra's algorithm
        distances = {start_node: 0}
        pq = [(0, start_node)]
        previous = {}
        
        while pq:
            current_dist, current = heapq.heappop(pq)
            
            if current == goal_node:
                break
                
            if current_dist > distances.get(current, float('inf')):
                continue
                
            for neighbor, edge_dist in graph[current]:
                new_dist = current_dist + edge_dist
                if neighbor not in distances or new_dist < distances[neighbor]:
                    distances[neighbor] = new_dist
                    previous[neighbor] = current
                    heapq.heappush(pq, (new_dist, neighbor))
        
        if goal_node not in distances:
            print(-1)
        else:
            # Reconstruct path
            path = []
            current = goal_node
            while current != start_node:
                if current not in previous:
                    print(-1)
                    break
                path.append(current)
                current = previous[current]
            path.append(start_node)
            path.reverse()
            
            # Output only the intersection points (not start or goal)
            intersection_path = []
            for point in path:
                if point in intersection_points:
                    intersection_path.append(point)
            
            # Print intersection points
            for point in intersection_path:
                print(f"{int(point[0])} {int(point[1])}")
            print(0)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

There are N mountains in a circle, called Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N in clockwise order. N is an odd number.
Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).
When Mountain i (1 \leq i \leq N) receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulates x liters of water (Dam 0 is Dam N).
One day, each of the mountains received a non-negative even number of liters of rain.
As a result, Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) accumulated a total of A_i liters of water.
Find the amount of rain each of the mountains received. We can prove that the solution is unique under the constraints of this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - All values in input are integers.
 - 3 \leq N \leq 10^5-1
 - N is an odd number.
 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 ... A_N

-----Output-----
Print N integers representing the number of liters of rain Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N received, in this order.

-----Sample Input-----
3
2 2 4

-----Sample Output-----
4 0 4

If we assume Mountain 1, 2, and 3 received 4, 0, and 4 liters of rain, respectively, it is consistent with this input, as follows:
 - Dam 1 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{0}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 2 should have accumulated \frac{0}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 3 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 4 liters of water.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (N is odd)
2. There are N dams between consecutive mountains
3. When mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, dams i-1 and i each get x liters
4. We're given the final water amounts in each dam (A_i)
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's denote:
- R_i = amount of rain mountain i received
- Since rain is even, R_i = 2x_i for some non-negative integer x_i

For dam i:
- Dam i gets water from mountain i (contribution: R_i/2) and from mountain i+1 (contribution: R_{i+1}/2)
- So: A_i = R_i/2 + R_{i+1}/2
- Therefore: 2A_i = R_i + R_{i+1}

This gives us a system of equations:
- 2A_1 = R_1 + R_2
- 2A_2 = R_2 + R_3
- 2A_3 = R_3 + R_4
- ...
- 2A_N = R_N + R_1

We have N equations with N unknowns (R_1, R_2, ..., R_N).

## Solving the System

From the equations:
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
- R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
- And so on...

We can see a pattern emerging. Since N is odd, we can use the last equation to get a constraint on R_1.

Let's work through the recurrence:
R_{i+1} = 2A_i - R_i

After N steps, we get back to R_1:
R_1 = 2A_N - R_{N} 

But R_{N} can be expressed in terms of R_1 through the recurrence. Let's find the pattern.

After working through the pattern, we can derive that:
R_1 = (2A_N - 2A_{N-1} + 2A_{N-2} - ... + 2A_1) / (1 + 2(-1)^{N-1}) but since N is odd, this simplifies...

Actually, let me solve it more systematically.

From the recurrence R_{i+1} = 2A_i - R_i, we can write:
R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1

The pattern is that the coefficient of R_1 alternates in sign: +, -, +, -, ...

After N steps (N is odd), we get:
R_{N+1} = 2A_N - R_N

But R_{N+1} = R_1 (since it's a circle), so:
R_1 = 2A_N - R_N

We can express R_N in terms of R_1:
R_N = 2A_{N-1} - R_{N-1}
R_{N-1} = 2A_{N-2} - R_{N-2}
...

When we substitute back, we get a closed form for R_1.

After working through the linear system, the solution is:
R_1 = (2(A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_{N-1}) - A_N) / 2

Wait, let me reconsider with a different approach.

From the system:
R_1 + R_2 = 2A_1
R_2 + R_3 = 2A_2
...
R_N + R_1 = 2A_N

Adding all equations: 2(R_1 + R_2 + ... + R_N) = 2(A_1 + A_2 + ... + A_N)
So sum(R_i) = sum(A_i)

Now, using the recurrence R_{i+1} = 2A_i - R_i, and since N is odd, we can solve for R_1.

The pattern for R_k in terms of R_1 is:
R_k = R_1 + 2(-1)^k * (A_{k-1} - A_{k-2} + A_{k-3} - ...)

After N steps, we get R_1 = 2A_N - R_N, and R_N can be expressed in terms of R_1.

Actually, I found a simpler pattern: since N is odd, the solution is:
R_1 = (A_1 + A_3 + A_5 + ... + A_{N-2}) - (A_2 + A_4 + A_6 + ... + A_{N-1}) + A_N

```python
# 2R_1 + 2 * alternating_sum = 2A_N
# R_1 = A_N - alternating_sum

# So R_1 = A_N - (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1})

# Simplifying: R_1 = A_N - A_1 + A_2 - A_3 + A_4 - ... + A_{N-1}

# This is our formula for R_1.

# Then we can compute all R_i using the recurrence.

n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate R_1
alternating_sum = 0
for i in range(n):
    if i % 2 == 0:  # even index (0,2,4,...) -> positive in A_1-A_2+A_3-..., so A_0, A_2, A_4, ... 
        alternating_sum += a[i]
    else:  # odd index (1,3,5,...) -> negative in A_1-A_2+A_3-..., so A_1, A_3, A_5, ...
        alternating_sum -= a[i]

# R_1 = A_N - (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1})
# But our alternating_sum is A_0 - A_1 + A_2 - A_3 + ... which is the opposite
# Actually, we want A_N - (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1})

# The alternating sum we want is: -A_1 + A_2 - A_3 + A_4 - ... + A_{N-1}
# Let's recalculate: for i from 0 to N-1, when i is even, coefficient is -1? 

# We want: R_1 = A_N - (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1})
# A_1 corresponds to index 0, A_2 to index 1, etc.

# So R_1 = a[n-1] - (a[0] - a[1] + a[2] - a[3] + ... - a[n-2])

# Calculate the alternating sum with correct signs for A_1 to A_{N-1}
alt_sum = 0
for i in range(n-1):  # A_1 to A_{N-1}
    if i % 2 == 0:  # i=0,2,4,... -> A_1, A_3, A_5, ... -> + for odd positions
        alt_sum += a[i]
    else:  # i=1,3,5,... -> A_2, A_4, A_6, ... -> - for even positions
        alt_sum -= a[i]

r1 = a[n-1] - alt_sum

# Now compute all rain amounts
rain = [0] * n
rain[0] = r1

for i in range(1, n):
    rain[i] = 2 * a[i-1] - rain[i-1]

print(' '.join(map(str, rain)))
```

Wait, I think I made an error in indexing. Let me verify with the sample:

Sample: N=3, A=[2,2,4]
We want rain=[4,0,4]

For N=3, alt_sum should be for i=0,1 (A_1, A_2):
- i=0 (even): +a[0] = +2
- i=1 (odd): -a[1] = -2
So alt_sum = 0

r1 = a[2] - alt_sum = 4 - 0 = 4

Then:
- rain[1] = 2*a[0] - rain[0] = 2*2 - 4 = 0
- rain[2] = 2*a[1] - rain[1] = 2*2 - 0 = 4

So rain = [4,0,4] ✓ Perfect!

```python
# Calculate alternating sum for A_1 to A_{N-1}: A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1}
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

There are N mountains in a circle, called Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N in clockwise order. N is an odd number.
Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).
When Mountain i (1 \leq i \leq N) receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulates x liters of water (Dam 0 is Dam N).
One day, each of the mountains received a non-negative even number of liters of rain.
As a result, Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) accumulated a total of A_i liters of water.
Find the amount of rain each of the mountains received. We can prove that the solution is unique under the constraints of this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - All values in input are integers.
 - 3 \leq N \leq 10^5-1
 - N is an odd number.
 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 ... A_N

-----Output-----
Print N integers representing the number of liters of rain Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N received, in this order.

-----Sample Input-----
3
2 2 4

-----Sample Output-----
4 0 4

If we assume Mountain 1, 2, and 3 received 4, 0, and 4 liters of rain, respectively, it is consistent with this input, as follows:
 - Dam 1 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{0}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 2 should have accumulated \frac{0}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 3 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 4 liters of water.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: Mountain 1, 2, ..., N)
2. There are N dams between mountains: Dam i is between Mountain i and Mountain i+1 (with Mountain N+1 = Mountain 1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each get x liters of water
4. We're given the total water accumulation in each dam (A_i) and need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Key Insights

Let's say Mountain i received R_i liters of rain. Since rain is even, R_i = 2x_i for some non-negative integer x_i.

When Mountain i receives 2x_i liters of rain:
- Dam i-1 gets x_i liters
- Dam i gets x_i liters

So for each dam i:
- Dam i gets water from Mountain i (x_i) and Mountain i+1 (x_{i+1})
- Therefore: A_i = x_i + x_{i+1}

This gives us the system:
- A_1 = x_1 + x_2
- A_2 = x_2 + x_3  
- A_3 = x_3 + x_4
- ...
- A_N = x_N + x_1

## Solving the System

We have N equations with N unknowns. Let's solve this system:

From A_1 = x_1 + x_2, we get: x_2 = A_1 - x_1
From A_2 = x_2 + x_3, we get: x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
From A_3 = x_3 + x_4, we get: x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1

We can see a pattern emerging. Let's continue and look at the last equation:

We want A_N = x_N + x_1

If we express all x_i in terms of x_1, we get:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
- x_5 = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

We can see that the pattern alternates between +x_1 and -x_1.

The general formula for x_i in terms of x_1 is:
- x_i = (-1)^(i+1) * x_1 + sum_{j=1}^{i-1} (-1)^(i-j) * A_j

For the last equation A_N = x_N + x_1:
- x_N = A_N - x_1

Substituting the pattern:
x_N = (-1)^(N+1) * x_1 + sum_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(N-j) * A_j

Since N is odd, (-1)^(N+1) = (-1)^(even) = 1, so:
x_N = x_1 + sum_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(N-j) * A_j

Setting this equal to A_N - x_1:
x_1 + sum_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(N-j) * A_j = A_N - x_1

Solving for x_1:
2x_1 = A_N - sum_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(N-j) * A_j

The sign pattern (-1)^(N-j) depends on whether (N-j) is odd or even:
- When N-j is even (j < N and j ≡ N (mod 2)), we have +A_j
- When N-j is odd (j < N and j ≡ N+1 (mod 2)), we have -A_j

Since N is odd:
- When j is even (j ≡ 0 (mod 2)), N-j is odd → -A_j
- When j is odd (j ≡ 1 (mod 2)), N-j is even → +A_j

Wait, let me reconsider. For odd N:
- N-j is even when j has the same parity as N (odd)
- N-j is odd when j has different parity from N (even)

Since N is odd:
- When j is odd, N-j is even → (-1)^(N-j) = +1
- When j is even, N-j is odd → (-1)^(N-j) = -1

So sum_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(N-j) * A_j = sum_{odd j} A_j - sum_{even j} A_j

Therefore:
2x_1 = A_N - (sum_{odd j} A_j - sum_{even j} A_j)

Let me verify this with the sample:
- N=3, A=[2,2,4]
- A_N = 4
- Sum of A_j for odd j (1,3): A_1 + A_3 = 2 + 4 = 6
- Sum of A_j for even j (2): A_2 = 2  
- So sum_{odd j} A_j - sum_{even j} A_j = 6 - 2 = 4
- 2x_1 = 4 - 4 = 0 → x_1 = 0

Wait, that gives x_1 = 0, so x_1 = 0, x_2 = 2, x_3 = 2, giving rain = [0,4,4], but expected is [4,0,4].

I think I made an error in the sign pattern. Let me reconsider the system:

From A_1 = x_1 + x_2 → x_2 = A_1 - x_1
From A_2 = x_2 + x_3 → x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
From A_3 = x_3 + x_1 → x_1 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1

So: 2x_1 = A_3 - A_2 + A_1
2x_1 = 4 - 2 + 2 = 4
x_1 = 2

Then:
x_2 = 2 - 2 = 0
x_3 = 2 - 0 = 2

So rain = 2*2, 2*0, 2*2 = 4, 0, 4 ✓

So the pattern is:
2x_1 = A_N - A_{N-1} + A_{N-2} - A_{N-3} + ... ± A_1

Since N is odd, the signs alternate starting with +A_1.
For A_1 to A_{N-1} in the pattern: +A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1}

In general: 2x_1 = sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(i+1) * A_i

For odd N: 
- i=1 (odd): +A_1
- i=2 (even): -A_2  
- i=3 (odd): +A_3
- etc.

This gives us x_1, and we can find all other x_i.

## Algorithm

1. Calculate x_1 using: 2x_1 = sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(i+1) * A_i
2. For each i from 1 to N: x_i = A_i - x_{i+1} (with x_{N+1} = x_1)

Actually, from A_i = x_i + x_{i+1}, we get x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i.

So we can compute x_1 first, then compute all others sequentially.

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate x_1 using the formula derived from the last equation
# 2x_1 = sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(i+1) * A_i
# For i=1: +A_1, i=2: -A_2, i=3: +A_3, etc.
x1 = 0
for i in range(N-1):  # i from 0 to N-2 (A[0] to A[N-2])
    if i % 2 == 0:  # i is even (0, 2, 4, ...)
        x1 += A[i]
    else:  # i is odd (1, 3, 5, ...)
        x1 -= A[i]

x1 //= 2  # x_1 is now the half of the rain for mountain 1

# Now compute all x_i values
x = [0] * N
x[0] = x1

# Use the relation A_i = x_i + x_{i+1} (with x_N + x_1)
for i in range(N-1):
    x[i+1] = A[i] - x[i]

# The last equation A_N = x_N + x_1 should hold due to our derivation
# But we need to verify the last term
# Actually, we have A[N-1] = x[N-1] + x[0], so x[N-1] = A[N-1] - x[0]

# The last element is already computed through the loop, so we're good
# But let's check if there's a consistency issue

# The rain amounts are 2*x[i] for each mountain
rain = [2 * x[i] for i in range(N)]

print(' '.join(map(str, rain)))
```

 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: Mountain 1, 2, ..., N)
2. We have N dams between mountains: Dam i is between Mountain i and Mountain i+1 (with Mountain N+1 = Mountain 1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulate x liters of water
4. We're given the final water accumulation in each dam (A_i)
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Key Insights

Let's say Mountain i received R_i liters of rain. Since the rain is even, R_i = 2x_i for some non-negative integer x_i.

When Mountain i receives 2x_i liters of rain:
- Dam (i-1) accumulates x_i liters
- Dam i accumulates x_i liters

So the total water in Dam j comes from:
- Mountain j+1 (which contributes to Dam j) 
- Mountain j (which contributes to Dam j)

Therefore: A_j = x_{j+1} + x_j

Where indices are modulo N (since it's a circle).

So we have the system:
A_1 = x_2 + x_1
A_2 = x_3 + x_2
...
A_N = x_1 + x_N

We can write this as:
x_1 + x_2 = A_1
x_2 + x_3 = A_2
...
x_N + x_1 = A_N

## Solving the System

This is a system of N equations with N variables. Notice that all equations are of the form x_i + x_{i+1} = A_i.

We can solve this by expressing x_i in terms of x_1:

From the first equation: x_2 = A_1 - x_1
From the second equation: x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
From the third equation: x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1

We can see a pattern emerging. Since N is odd, when we go through all N equations and come back to x_1, we should get a consistent equation.

The general pattern is:
x_{i} = (-1)^{i-1} * x_1 + (sum of alternating signs of A values)

Let me work through the pattern:
- x_1 = x_1
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = (A_2 - A_1) + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = (A_3 - A_2 + A_1) - x_1
- x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - (A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1) = (A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1) + x_1

For odd N, we have the last equation x_N + x_1 = A_N.

Since N is odd, after going through N steps, we get back to x_1 with a sign pattern.

The pattern shows that x_{k} = (-1)^{k-1} * x_1 + C_k, where C_k is a constant.

For the last equation (x_N + x_1 = A_N), we substitute:
x_N + x_1 = A_N
[(-1)^{N-1} * x_1 + C_N] + x_1 = A_N

Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^{N-1} = 1.
So: (1 * x_1 + C_N) + x_1 = A_N
2x_1 + C_N = A_N
2x_1 = A_N - C_N
x_1 = (A_N - C_N) / 2

Now I need to find C_N. Looking at the pattern, C_k is the alternating sum of A values.

Actually, let me reconsider. The constant term C_k for x_k is the alternating sum of A values from A_1 to A_{k-1}.

For x_1: C_1 = 0
For x_2: C_2 = A_1  
For x_3: C_3 = A_2 - A_1
For x_4: C_4 = A_3 - A_2 + A_1
For x_5: C_5 = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1

So C_k = sum_{j=1}^{k-1} (-1)^{k-1-j} * A_j

For x_N, C_N = sum_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-1-j} * A_j

Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^{N-1-j} = (-1)^{-j} = (-1)^j (since (-1)^{-j} = (-1)^j)

Actually, (-1)^{N-1-j} = (-1)^{even-j} = (-1)^{-j} = (-1)^j

So C_N = sum_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^j * A_j

From the last equation: 2x_1 + C_N = A_N
So x_1 = (A_N - C_N) / 2

Then we can find all other x_i using the recurrence relation.

## Implementation

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# We need to find x_1 such that the system is consistent
# x_i + x_{i+1} = A_i for all i

# From the pattern, x_1 = (A_N - C_N) / 2
# where C_N = sum_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^j * A_j

C_N = 0
for j in range(N-1):
    if j % 2 == 0:  # (-1)^j = 1 when j is even
        C_N += A[j]
    else:  # (-1)^j = -1 when j is odd
        C_N -= A[j]

x1 = (A[N-1] - C_N) // 2

# Now build all x_i
x = [0] * N
x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, N):
    # x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i
    x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1]

# The rain amount is 2 * x_i for mountain i
result = [2 * x[i] for i in range(N)]

print(*result)
```

Wait, let me verify this with the sample:
- N = 3, A = [2, 2, 4]

C_N = for j=0: (-1)^0 * A[0] = 1*2 = 2
      for j=1: (-1)^1 * A[1] = -1*2 = -2
So C_N = 2 - 2 = 0

x1 = (A[2] - C_N) // 2 = (4 - 0) // 2 = 2

x[0] = 2
x[1] = A[0] - x[0] = 2 - 2 = 0  
x[2] = A[1] - x[1] = 2 - 0 = 2

Rain amounts: 2*2=4, 2*0=0, 2*2=4 → [4, 0, 4]

This matches the expected sample output! ✓

```python
        C_N += A[j]
    else:
        C_N -= A[j]
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (odd number N)
2. There are N dams between mountains (Dam i between Mountain i and i+1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each get x liters
4. We're given the final water amounts A_i in each dam, and need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Key Insights

Let's say Mountain i received R_i liters of rain (all even numbers).

Since R_i is even, let R_i = 2x_i, so x_i = R_i/2.

When Mountain i receives 2x_i liters:
- Dam i-1 gets x_i liters
- Dam i gets x_i liters

So for Dam j:
- Dam j gets water from Mountain j (as x_j) and Mountain j+1 (as x_{j+1})
- Therefore: A_j = x_j + x_{j+1}

Since R_j = 2x_j, we have x_j = R_j/2.

## Mathematical Formulation

For each dam j:
A_j = x_j + x_{j+1}

We need to find x_1, x_2, ..., x_N where:
A_j = x_j + x_{j+1} for all j (with N+1 meaning 1)

This is a system of equations. We can solve it using the fact that N is odd.

## Solving the System

From A_j = x_j + x_{j+1}, we get:
x_{j+1} = A_j - x_j

We can express x_2 in terms of x_1:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
- And so on...

We see a pattern: the signs alternate and depend on the position.

Since N is odd, we can go all the way to x_{N+1} = x_1 (circularity).

So: x_1 = A_N - x_N

Let's work out the general pattern:
x_{k+1} = A_k - x_k

After N steps, we get back to x_1:
x_1 = A_{N} - x_N

But we can also express x_N in terms of x_1:
x_N = A_{N-1} - x_{N-1}
Continuing this chain, we eventually express x_N in terms of x_1.

Since N is odd, the pattern will give us an equation that can solve for x_1.

## Pattern Analysis

Let me trace through the expansion:
x_1 = x_1
x_2 = A_1 - x_1
x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

For odd indices, the coefficient of x_1 is positive, for even indices it's negative.

Since N is odd, when we reach x_{N+1} = x_1:
x_1 = A_N - x_N

And x_N has the form: x_N = (sum of A's with alternating signs) + (-1)^(N-1) * x_1

Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^(N-1) = 1.

So: x_1 = A_N - [(sum with alternating signs) + x_1]
x_1 = A_N - (sum with alternating signs) - x_1
2x_1 = A_N - (sum with alternating signs)
x_1 = (A_N - sum with alternating signs) / 2

The sum with alternating signs: A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + (-1)^{N-1} A_1

Wait, let me rework this more systematically.

## Correct Pattern

From the recurrence x_{k+1} = A_k - x_k, after N steps:
x_1 = A_N - x_N

But x_N = A_{N-1} - x_{N-1}
And so on...

The pattern shows that x_k = (some alternating sum) + (-1)^(k-1) * x_1

For k=N: x_N = (alternating sum) + (-1)^{N-1} * x_1

Since N is odd, (-1)^{N-1} = 1, so:
x_N = (alternating sum) + x_1

Then: x_1 = A_N - x_N = A_N - [(alternating sum) + x_1]
x_1 = A_N - (alternating sum) - x_1
2x_1 = A_N - (alternating sum)
x_1 = (A_N - alternating sum) / 2

The alternating sum: A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + (-1)^{N-2} A_1

This is equal to: A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_{N-1} - A_N (since signs start with positive for A_1)

Actually, let me recompute the alternating sum in terms of A_1 to A_{N-1}.

After working through the pattern, I find that:
x_1 = (A_N - (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_{N-1})) / 2

Let me verify with the sample:
N=3, A=[2,2,4]
x_1 = (4 - (2 - 2)) / 2 = (4 - 0) / 2 = 2
So R_1 = 2 * x_1 = 4
x_2 = A_1 - x_1 = 2 - 2 = 0
So R_2 = 0
x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = 2 - 0 = 2
So R_3 = 4

This gives [4,0,4] which matches the expected output!

## Algorithm

1. Calculate alternating sum S = A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_{N-1}
2. x_1 = (A_N - S) / 2
3. For each i from 1 to N: x_i = A_{i-1} - x_{i-1} (with A_0 = A_N)
4. Rain for mountain i is 2 * x_i

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate alternating sum S = A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_{N-1}
s = 0
for i in range(n - 1):  # i from 0 to n-2 (A_1 to A_{N-1})
    if i % 2 == 0:
        s += a[i]
    else:
        s -= a[i]

# x_1 = (A_N - s) / 2
x1 = (a[n-1] - s) // 2

# Calculate all x_i values
x = [0] * n
x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, n):
    x[i] = a[i-1] - x[i-1]

# Rain for each mountain is 2 * x_i
result = [2 * xi for xi in x]

print(' '.join(map(str, result)))
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (odd number N)
2. Between each pair of consecutive mountains, there's a dam
3. When mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, dams i-1 and i each accumulate x liters
4. We're given the total water accumulated in each dam (A_i)
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's say mountain i received R_i liters of rain (where R_i is even).

For dam i:
- It gets R_{i-1}/2 from mountain i-1
- It gets R_i/2 from mountain i

So: A_i = R_{i-1}/2 + R_i/2 = (R_{i-1} + R_i)/2

Rearranging: R_{i-1} + R_i = 2A_i

This gives us a system of N equations:
- R_N + R_1 = 2A_1 (with R_0 = R_N)
- R_1 + R_2 = 2A_2
- R_2 + R_3 = 2B_3
- ...
- R_{N-1} + R_N = 2A_N

## Solving the System

Since N is odd, we can solve this systematically.

From the equation R_{i-1} + R_i = 2A_i, we can express R_i in terms of R_{i-1}:
R_i = 2A_i - R_{i-1}

Let's trace through the pattern:
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
- R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1

We can see a pattern emerging. Let's continue to N:
- R_{N} = 2A_{N-1} - R_{N-1}

But we also have the constraint that R_N + R_1 = 2A_1.

Substituting R_N from the recurrence:
R_N = 2A_{N-1} - R_{N-1}

So: (2A_{N-1} - R_{N-1}) + R_1 = 2A_1

This gives us: R_1 = 2A_1 - 2A_{N-1} + R_{N-1}

Since we have a cyclic dependency, we can express everything in terms of R_1.

For odd N, after N steps we'll get back to R_1, and the constraints will give us a specific value.

Actually, let me reconsider the pattern more systematically.

From R_i = 2A_i - R_{i-1}, we can write:
R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
R_5 = 2A_4 - R_4 = 2A_4 - (2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1

R_1 = 2A_{N-1} - R_{N-1}

Actually, let me implement the recurrence and use the fact that N is odd to get a closed form.

Looking at the pattern more carefully:
For position i in the cycle (1-indexed), we have:
R_1 = R_1
R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
...

For odd N, when we reach R_N and use R_N + R_1 = 2A_1, we get a constraint on R_1.

After working through this, I find that the solution is:
R_i = 2 * (A_{i-1} - A_{i-2} + A_{i-3} - ... + (-1)^{i-1} * A_1) where the sign alternates.

However, a simpler approach is to notice that the system has a unique solution and we can solve it with the recurrence, constrained by the cyclic nature.

For odd N, the pattern of signs in the alternating sum will work out such that the constraint is satisfied when we sum appropriately.

After analysis, the correct approach is:
- Use the recurrence R_i = 2A_i - R_{i-1} for i from 1 to N
- The constraint R_N + R_1 = 2A_1 gives us a specific value for R_1
- For odd N, the alternating sum gives us R_1 = 2(A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - ... + A_{N-1}) when considering the pattern

If R_1 = x, then:
R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1 = 4 - x

R_i + R_{i+1} = 2A_i for i=1 to N (with R_{N+1} = R_1)

Solving:

- R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1

R_1 = 2A_N - R_N

- R_1 = A_N - A_{N-1} + A_{N-2} - A_{N-3} + ... + A_2 - A_1 + A_1

- For i=N: (-1)^{N-N} = (-1)^0 = 1
- For i=N-1: (-1)^{1} = -1
- For i=N-2: (-1)^{2} = 1
- ...

So R_1 = sum_{i=1}^N (-1)^{N-i} * A_i

This gives R_1 = A_N - A_{N-1} + A_{N-2} - ... + A_1

For N=3: A_3 - A_2 + A_1 = 4 - 2 + 2 = 4 ✓

R_i = 2A_{i-1} - R_{i-1} for i=2,3,...,N

Wait, the equation for dam i is: R_i + R_{i+1} = 2A_i

So: R_{i+1} = 2A_i - R_i

Therefore, we can compute:
- R_1 = sum_{j=1}^N (-1)^{N-j} * A_j
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2
- ...
- R_N = 2A_{N-1} - R_{N-1}

And we need to verify that R_1 + R_N = 2A_N, but due to the construction, it should hold.

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Compute R_1 using the alternating sum formula
# R_1 = A_n - A_{n-1} + A_{n-2} - ... + A_1
# This is sum_{j=0}^{n-1} (-1)^j * A[n-1-j]
r1 = 0
for i in range(n):
    # i=0: j=0, (-1)^0 * A[n-1] = A[n-1]
    # i=1: j=1, (-1)^1 * A[n-2] = -A[n-2]
    # So for j from 0 to n-1: (-1)^j * A[n-1-j]
    sign = 1 if i % 2 == 0 else -1
    r1 += sign * a[n - 1 - i]

# Now compute the rest using recurrence
rain = [0] * n
rain[0] = r1

for i in range(1, n):
    # R_{i+1} = 2*A_i - R_i
    # For dam i+1: R_{i+1} + R_{i+2} = 2*A_{i+1}
    # But we have: R_{i+1} = 2*A_i - R_i
    rain[i] = 2 * a[i-1] - rain[i-1]
    
# For the last one, we need to handle the circular dependency
# Actually, the recurrence should work because of the alternating sum constraint
# But let me verify: for the last one, from dam N: R_N + R_1 = 2*A_N
# We have R_N = 2*A_{N-1} - R_{N-1}
# And we computed R_1 using the constraint that should ensure this is satisfied

# However, there's an off-by-one issue in the indexing
# Let me re-index: 
# For i from 1 to n: R_i + R_{i+1} = 2*A_i (with R_{n+1} = R_1)
# So for i=1: R_1 + R_2 = 2*A_1
# i=2: R_2 + R_3 = 2*A_2
# ...
# i=n: R_n + R_1 = 2*A_n

# So R_2 = 2*A_1 - R_1
# R_3 = 2*A_2 - R_2
# R_4 = 2*A_3 - R_3
# ...
# R_n = 2*A_{n-1} - R_{n-1}

# So we can compute all directly:

rain = [0] * n
rain[0] = r1

for i in range(1, n):
    rain[i] = 2 * a[i-1] - rain[i-1]

# But we need to ensure the last constraint is satisfied
# R_n + R_1 should equal 2*a[n-1]
# Let me verify:

# Actually, we should use the constraint that gives us R_1
# After computing all, we verify R_n + R_1 should be 2*a[n-1]

# But due to the construction of R_1, it should be satisfied.

print(' '.join(map(str, rain)))
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

There are N mountains in a circle, called Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N in clockwise order. N is an odd number.
Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).
When Mountain i (1 \leq i \leq N) receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulates x liters of water (Dam 0 is Dam N).
One day, each of the mountains received a non-negative even number of liters of rain.
As a result, Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) accumulated a total of A_i liters of water.
Find the amount of rain each of the mountains received. We can prove that the solution is unique under the constraints of this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - All values in input are integers.
 - 3 \leq N \leq 10^5-1
 - N is an odd number.
 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 ... A_N

-----Output-----
Print N integers representing the number of liters of rain Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N received, in this order.

-----Sample Input-----
3
2 2 4

-----Sample Output-----
4 0 4

If we assume Mountain 1, 2, and 3 received 4, 0, and 4 liters of rain, respectively, it is consistent with this input, as follows:
 - Dam 1 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{0}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 2 should have accumulated \frac{0}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 3 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 4 liters of water.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: 1, 2, ..., N)
2. There are N dams between mountains: Dam i is between Mountain i and Mountain i+1 (with Mountain N+1 = Mountain 1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulate x liters of water
4. We're given the final amount of water in each dam A_i
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's say Mountain i received R_i liters of rain (where R_i is even).

When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, it contributes x liters to Dam i-1 and x liters to Dam i.

So for Mountain i receiving R_i = 2x_i liters:
- Dam i-1 gets x_i = R_i / 2 liters
- Dam i gets x_i = R_i / 2 liters

Let's define R_i = amount of rain on Mountain i.

For Dam j:
- Dam j gets contributions from Mountain j (which contributes to Dam j) and Mountain j+1 (which contributes to Dam j)
- The contribution from Mountain j is R_j / 2
- The contribution from Mountain j+1 is R_{j+1} / 2

So: A_j = R_j / 2 + R_{j+1} / 2

Therefore: 2A_j = R_j + R_{j+1}

This gives us the key equation: R_{j+1} = 2A_j - R_j

## Solution Strategy

We have the equation: R_{j+1} = 2A_j - R_j for j = 1, 2, ..., N

Since the mountains are in a circle, we need to handle the wrap-around: R_{N+1} = R_1.

Let's write this system:
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2
- ...
- R_N = 2A_{N-1} - R_{N-1}
- R_1 = 2A_N - R_N

We can solve this recurrence relation by expressing everything in terms of R_1.

From the equations:
R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1

I notice a pattern: the coefficients of R_1 alternate in sign, and we have a pattern in terms of A values.

Let me solve this system more systematically.

From R_{j+1} = 2A_j - R_j, we can write:
R_{j+1} + R_j = 2A_j

This is a linear recurrence relation.

Let me work through the pattern:
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
- R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
- R_5 = 2A_4 - R_4 = 2A_4 - (2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1

I see the pattern: the coefficient of R_1 alternates with sign, and the A terms follow a pattern.

Since N is odd, when we go from R_1 to R_{N+1} = R_1, we'll have an even number of steps, and the sign of R_1 will be negative in the final equation.

Actually, let me derive the general formula.

After working through the pattern, I find that:
R_j = (-1)^{j-1} * R_1 + 2 * Σ (from k=1 to j-1) of (-1)^{j-1-k} * A_k

For j=1: R_1 = R_1 (trivial)
For j=2: R_2 = -R_1 + 2A_1
For j=3: R_3 = R_1 + 2(A_2 - A_1)
For j=4: R_4 = -R_1 + 2(A_3 - A_2 + A_1)

The pattern shows that after N steps (with N odd), we get R_1 in terms of R_1.

Specifically, since N is odd:
R_1 = 2A_N - R_N

But R_N can be expressed in terms of R_1 and the A values.

Actually, let's just implement the recurrence and use the fact that the system is consistent.

Since N is odd, the closed form will work out such that we can solve for R_1.

The key insight: we can write R_{j} in terms of R_1, and then use the cyclic condition to solve for R_1.

After working through the math, the pattern is:
R_j = (-1)^{j-1} * R_1 + 2 * Σ (k=1 to j-1) of (-1)^{j-1-k} * A_k

For the cyclic condition R_1 = 2A_N - R_N, we can solve for R_1.

Let me implement the straightforward approach with the recurrence:

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# We'll solve using the recurrence relation
# We can express all R_i in terms of R_1
# R_{i+1} = 2*A_i - R_i

# Let's solve for R_1 first
# After N steps, we should get back to R_1
# R_2 = 2*A_1 - R_1
# R_3 = 2*A_2 - R_2 = 2*A_2 - (2*A_1 - R_1) = 2*A_2 - 2*A_1 + R_1
# R_4 = 2*A_3 - R_3 = 2*A_3 - (2*A_2 - 2*A_1 + R_1) = 2*A_3 - 2*A_2 + 2*A_1 - R_1
# ...

# The pattern: R_j = (-1)^(j-1) * R_1 + 2 * sum of alternating A values

# Since N is odd, when we get to R_{N+1} = R_1, the coefficient of R_1 will be (-1)^N = -1
# So: R_1 = (-1)^N * R_1 + 2 * (sum of alternating coefficients * A)

# For odd N: (-1)^N = -1
# So: R_1 = -R_1 + 2 * S
# Therefore: 2*R_1 = 2*S
# So: R_1 = S

# Where S is the alternating sum of A values with positive sign for the first, negative for the second, etc.

# Actually, let me compute the alternating sum correctly:

# R_1 = (-1)^N * R_1 + 2 * (alternating sum of A_i)
# For odd N: (-1)^N = -1
# So: R_1 = -R_1 + 2 * S
# 2*R_1 = 2*S
# R_1 = S

# What is S? 
# S = A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... ± A_N
# Since N is odd, the last term has positive sign

S = 0
for i in range(N):
    if i % 2 == 0:
        S += A[i]
    else:
        S -= A[i]

R = [0] * N
R[0] = S

# Now compute the rest using the recurrence
for i in range(N-1):
    R[i+1] = 2 * A[i] - R[i]

print(*R)
```

- Dam 1: R_0/2 + R_1/2 = 4/2 + 0/2 = 2 ✓
- Dam 2: R_1/2 + R_2/2 = 0/2 + 4/2 = 2 ✓  
- Dam 3: R_2/2 + R_0/2 = 4/2 + 4/2 = 4 ✓

Perfect! It works.

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate the alternating sum S = A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - ... + A_N
S = 0
for i in range(N):
    if i % 2 == 0:
        S += A[i]
    else:
        S -= A[i]

# The rain on Mountain 1 is S
rain = [0] * N
rain[0] = S

# Calculate the rest using the recurrence R_{i+1} = 2*A_i - R_i
for i in range(N-1):
    rain[i+1] = 2 * A[i] - rain[i]

print(*rain)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (N is odd)
2. There are N dams between consecutive mountains
3. When mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, dams i-1 and i each accumulate x liters
4. We're given the total water accumulated in each dam A_i
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's say mountain i received R_i liters of rain (which is even, so R_i = 2x_i).

For dam i:
- It gets water from mountain i (2x_i/2 = x_i) and mountain i+1 (2x_{i+1}/2 = x_{i+1})
- So A_i = x_i + x_{i+1}

This gives us the system:
- A_1 = x_1 + x_2
- A_2 = x_2 + x_3  
- A_3 = x_3 + x_4
- ...
- A_N = x_N + x_1

We need to solve for x_1, x_2, ..., x_N, then multiply each by 2 to get R_i = 2x_i.

## Solving the System

We have N equations with N variables. Let's solve it:

From the equations:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
- and so on...

Following this pattern, we can see that:
- x_i = (-1)^(i-1) * x_1 + (some constant)

Since N is odd, when we reach the last equation:
- A_N = x_N + x_1

We can express all x_i in terms of x_1, substitute into the last equation, and solve for x_1.

## Pattern Recognition

After working through the algebra, we find that:
- x_i = (-1)^(i-1) * x_1 + (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + (-1)^(i-2) * A_{i-1})

For the last equation A_N = x_N + x_1:
- A_N = [(-1)^(N-1) * x_1 + (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + (-1)^(N-2) * A_{N-1})] + x_1

Since N is odd, (-1)^(N-1) = 1, so:
- A_N = x_1 + (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + (-1)^(N-2) * A_{N-1}) + x_1
- A_N = 2x_1 + (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + (-1)^(N-2) * A_{N-1})

Solving for x_1:
- 2x_1 = A_N - (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + (-1)^(N-2) * A_{N-1})
- x_1 = (A_N - (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + (-1)^(N-2) * A_{N-1})) / 2

## Algorithm

1. Calculate the alternating sum S = A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + (-1)^(N-2) * A_{N-1}
2. Calculate x_1 = (A_N - S) / 2
3. Calculate each x_i using the pattern: x_i = (-1)^(i-1) * x_1 + (alternating sum up to i-1)
4. Multiply each x_i by 2 to get rain amounts

Wait, let me reconsider the pattern more carefully.

Actually, let me implement a simpler solution by working through the recurrence relation.

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# We need to find x_i such that A_i = x_i + x_{i+1} (with indices mod n)
# This gives us a system we can solve

# Let's express all x_i in terms of x_1
# x_2 = A_1 - x_1
# x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
# x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
# x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - (A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1) = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

# So the pattern is: x_i = (-1)^(i-1) * x_1 + (alternating sum)

# For the last equation: A_n = x_n + x_1
# Substituting the pattern:
# A_n = [(-1)^(n-1) * x_1 + alternating_sum] + x_1
# Since n is odd, (-1)^(n-1) = 1
# A_n = x_1 + alternating_sum + x_1 = 2x_1 + alternating_sum
# So x_1 = (A_n - alternating_sum) / 2

# Calculate alternating sum (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_{n-1})
alternating_sum = 0
for i in range(n - 1):
    if i % 2 == 0:  # even index (0,2,4,...) means positive
        alternating_sum += a[i]
    else:  # odd index (1,3,5,...) means negative
        alternating_sum -= a[i]

# Calculate x_1
x1 = (a[n-1] - alternating_sum) // 2

# Calculate all x_i
x = [0] * n
x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, n):
    # x_i = x_{i-1} - (A_{i-1} - A_{i-2}) + 2 * A_{i-2} (this is messy)
    # Better: from A_{i-1} = x_{i-1} + x_i, so x_i = A_{i-1} - x_{i-1}
    x[i] = a[i-1] - x[i-1]

# Convert to rain (multiply by 2)
result = [2 * xi for xi in x]

print(' '.join(map(str, result)))
```

Wait, I made an error in the recurrence. Let me fix this.

Actually, from A_i = x_i + x_{i+1}, we get x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i.

- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1

And so on. But we know from the last equation that it should be consistent.

The recurrence x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i is correct, but we need to ensure we get the right value for x_1.

Since we have a circular system, we should use the consistency condition from the last equation.

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# The system is: A_i = x_i + x_{i+1} for all i (indices mod n)
# So x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i

# We can express x_2, x_3, ..., x_n in terms of x_1
# x_2 = A_1 - x_1
# x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
# x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
# ...

# So x_i alternates with sign: x_i = (-1)^(i-1) * x_1 + constant

# For the last equation: A_n = x_n + x_1
# x_n = A_{n-1} - x_{n-1}
# We need to express x_n in terms of x_1

# Let's build the pattern:
# x_1 = x_1
# x_2 = A_1 - x_1
# x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
# x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
# x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

# So pattern: x_i = (-1)^(i-1) * x_1 + (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ...)

# For i=n: x_n = (-1)^(n-1) * x_1 + (alternating sum from 1 to n-1)
# Since n is odd, (-1)^(n-1) = 1, so:
# x_n = x_1 + alternating_sum

# From the last equation: A_n = x_n + x_1 = (x_1 + alternating_sum) + x_1 = 2x_1 + alternating_sum
# So 2x_1 = A_n - alternating_sum
# x_1 = (A_n - alternating_sum) // 2

# Calculate alternating sum: A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + (-1)^(n-2) * A_{n-1}
alternating_sum = 0
for i in range(n - 1):
    if i % 2 == 0:  # even index (0,2,4,...) in 0-based range means positive
        alternating_sum += a[i]
    else:  # odd index (1,3,5,...) means negative
        alternating_sum -= a[i]

x1 = (a[n-1] - alternating_sum) // 2

# Now compute all x values using recurrence
x = [0] * n
x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, n):
    x[i] = a[i-1] - x[i-1]

# Convert to rain (multiply by 2)
result = [2 * xi for xi in x]

print(' '.join(map(str, result)))
```

Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (N is odd)
2. There are N dams between consecutive mountains
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam (i-1) and Dam i each get x liters
4. We're given the accumulated water in each dam A_i
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's say Mountain i received R_i liters of rain (all R_i are even).

From the problem:
- Mountain i receives 2x liters → Dam (i-1) and Dam i each get x liters
- So if Mountain i receives R_i liters, then R_i/2 liters go to Dam (i-1) and Dam i

Therefore:
- Dam 1 accumulates: R_1/2 + R_2/2
- Dam 2 accumulates: R_2/2 + R_3/2  
- Dam 3 accumulates: R_3/2 + R_4/2
- ...
- Dam N accumulates: R_N/2 + R_1/2

We're given A_1, A_2, ..., A_N for dams.

So we have the system:
A_1 = R_1/2 + R_2/2
A_2 = R_2/2 + R_3/2
...
A_N = R_N/2 + R_1/2

Let's multiply all equations by 2:
2A_1 = R_1 + R_2
2A_2 = R_2 + R_3
...
2A_N = R_N + R_1

## Solving the System

We have:
- 2A_1 = R_1 + R_2
- 2A_2 = R_2 + R_3
- 2A_3 = R_3 + R_4
- ...
- 2A_N = R_N + R_1

Let me work with these equations. I can express each R_i in terms of the previous ones.

From the first equation: R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
From the second: R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
From the third: R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1

I notice a pattern emerging. Let me check the last equation to find R_1.

Since N is odd, we can use the fact that the system closes after N steps.

From the pattern, I can see that:
- R_1 = R_1
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1  
- R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
- R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
- R_5 = 2A_4 - R_4 = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1

For an odd N, if I go through N equations, I should get back to R_1.

Actually, let me use a different approach. Adding all N equations:
Sum of (R_1 + R_2) + (R_2 + R_3) + ... + (R_N + R_1) = Sum of 2A_i

Left side: (R_1 + R_1) + (R_2 + R_2) + ... + (R_N + R_N) = 2(R_1 + R_2 + ... + R_N)
Right side: 2(A_1 + A_2 + ... + A_N)

So: 2(sum R_i) = 2(sum A_i)
Therefore: sum R_i = sum A_i

Now, let me find a formula for R_1.

From the equations:
R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
R_5 = 2A_4 - R_4 = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1

I see that for odd indices, the pattern has +R_1, for even indices it has -R_1.

Since N is odd, when I get to R_N, I can use the last equation:
2A_N = R_N + R_1

The pattern is: R_k = 2A_{k-1} - 2A_{k-2} + 2A_{k-3} - ... ± R_1

2A_N = R_N + R_1

R_1 = R_1

R_4 = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
R_5 = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1

For odd N, the pattern for R_i has alternating signs of R_1.

R_i = R_1 + 2 * Σ from j=1 to i-1 of (-1)^{j+1} * A_{(j-1) mod N}

## Verification with Sample

Sample: N=3, A=[2,2,4]

Equations:
- 2A_1 = R_1 + R_2 → 4 = R_1 + R_2
- 2A_2 = R_2 + R_3 → 4 = R_2 + R_3

Then R_2 = 4 - 4 = 0, R_3 = 4

So R = [4, 0, 4] ✓

## Algorithm

I can use the recurrence:
- Start with R_1 as unknown

- R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1

In general: R_i = (-1)^(i-1) * R_1 + 2 * Σ from k=1 to i-1 of (-1)^(i-1-k) * A_{(k-1) mod N}

For the last equation (i=N): 2A_N = R_N + R_1

So: R_N = 2A_N - R_1

Actually (-1)^(N-1-j) = (-1)^(even-j) = (-1)^(-j) = (-1)^j (since (-1)^even = 1).

So R_N = R_1 + 2 * Σ_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^j * A_{(j-1) mod N}

But A_{(j-1) mod N} = A_{j-1} for j=1 to N-1, since j-1 < N-1 < N.

So R_N = R_1 + 2 * Σ_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^j * A_{j-1}

2R_1 = 2A_N - 2 * Σ_{k=0}^{N-2} (-1)^{k+1} * A_k

2R_1 = 2A_N + 2 * Σ_{k=0}^{N-2} (-1)^k * A_k

So R_1 = A_N + Σ_{k=0}^{N-2} (-1)^k * A_k

Let me write A in 1-indexed: A_1, A_2, ..., A_N

The sum is A_N + (A_0 + (-A_1) + A_2 + (-A_3) + ... + (-1)^{N-2} * A_{N-2})

Since A_0 is A_N (circular), in our indexing A_k corresponds to A_{k+1} (1-indexed), but k from 0 to N-2.

Actually in 1-indexed: 
- k=0 → A_1
- k=1 → A_2
- ...
- k=N-2 → A_{N-1}

So sum is A_N + (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + (-1)^{N-2} * A_{N-1})

Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^{N-2} = (-1)^{odd} = -1? Wait N-2 = odd-2 = odd-2 = odd if N=odd?

N is odd, so N-2 is odd. So (-1)^{N-2} = -1.

The pattern is alternating starting with +A_1: +A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1}

Since N-1 is even (N odd), the last term is A_{N-1} with positive sign? No:

The pattern: +A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... 
N-1 is even, so the last term is +A_{N-1} if N-1 is odd, but N-1 is even.

Let me check N=3:
N-1=2 (even)
Sum: A_3 + (A_1 - A_2)

So R_1 = A_3 + (A_1 - A_2) = 4 + (2 - 2) = 4 ✓

Perfect! So the formula is:
R_1 = A_N + Σ_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{k+1} * A_k

For k from 1 to N-1, (-1)^{k+1}:
- k=1: (-1)^2 = +1 → +A_1
- k=2: (-1)^3 = -1 → -A_2  
- k=3: (-1)^4 = +1 → +A_3
- ...

So R_1 = A_N + A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_{N-1}  (since N-1 is even)

Wait, N-1 is even, so the last term is +A_{N-1}? Let me check:

For k=N-1, (-1)^{(N-1)+1} = (-1)^N = -1 since N is odd.

Oh! I made a mistake.

(-1)^{(N-1)+1} = (-1)^N = -1 since N is odd.

So the sign for A_{N-1} is -1.

So R_1 = A_N + (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1})

And since N-1 is even, it ends with -A_{N-1}.

This matches our sample: N=3, A_1=2, A_2=2, A_3=4
R_1 = 4 + (2 - 2) = 4 ✓

Now I can compute all R_i using the pattern:
- R_1 = A_N + Σ_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{k+1} * A_k
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2
- ...
- R_i = 2A_{i-1} - R_{i-1} for i > 1

But I need to be careful with indices.

Actually, from the pattern:
R_i = 2A_{i-1} - R_{i-1} for i = 2,3,...,N
And R_1 as computed above.

But let me verify with the sample:
N=3, A=[2,2,4]
R_1 = 4 + (2 - 2) = 4
R_2 = 2*A_1 - R_1 = 4 - 4 = 0
R_3 = 2*A_2 - R_2 = 4 - 0 = 4

Perfect! So the algorithm is:

1. Compute R_1 = A_N + A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + (-1)^{N+1} * A_{N-1}
   i.e., R_1 = A_N + Σ_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{k+1} * A_k

2. For i from 2 to N: R_i = 2 * A_{i-1} - R_{i-1}

Wait, need to be careful with indexing in code.

In 1-indexed:
- A_1, A_2, ..., A_N
- R_1 = A_N + Σ_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(k+1) * A_k
- For i from 2 to N: R_i = 2 * A_{i-1} - R_{i-1}

But when i=N, R_N = 2 * A_{N-1} - R_{N-1}

And we need to ensure this satisfies 2A_N = R_N + R_1.

Let me verify with sample:
- R_1 = 4 + (2 - 2) = 4
- R_2 = 2*A_1 - R_1 = 4 - 4 = 0
- R_3 = 2*A_2 - R_2 = 4 - 0 = 4
- Check: 2A_3 = 8, R_3 + R_1 = 4 + 4 = 8 ✓

Perfect!

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate R_1
r1 = a[-1]  # a[-1] is A_N
for k in range(n - 1):
    if k % 2 == 0:  # k+1 is odd, so (-1)^(k+1) = +1 when k is even
        r1 += a[k]
    else:  # k+1 is even, so (-1)^(k+1) = -1 when k is odd
        r1 -= a[k]

# Calculate R_i for i from 2 to n
r = [0] * n
r[0] = r1  # R_1

for i in range(1, n):
    # R_{i+1} = 2 * A_i - R_i
    r[i] = 2 * a[i-1] - r[i-1]

print(*r)
```

Wait, let me double-check the alternating sum.

For k from 0 to n-2 (since k=1 to N-1 in 1-indexed, so k=0 to n-2 in 0-indexed):
- k=0: (-1)^(0+1) = (-1)^1 = -1? No, in the formula I have R_1 = A_N + Σ_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(k+1) * A_k

In 0-indexed:
A_k corresponds to A_{k+1} in 1-indexed.

So for k from 0 to n-2:
- We want (-1)^(k+1) where k is 0-indexed.

For k=0: (-1)^(0+1) = -1 → -A_1
For k=1: (-1)^(1+1) = +1 → +A_2
For k=2: (-1)^(2+1) = -1 → -A_3
...

This is wrong! I want +A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ...

So I want (-1)^(k) where k starts at 0:
- k=0: +A_1 (since (-1)^0 = 1)
- k=1: -A_2 (since (-1)^1 = -1)
- k=2: +A_3 (since (-1)^2 = 1)

So I should use (-1)^k for k=0 to n-2.

Therefore, R_1 = a[n-1] + sum_{k=0}^{n-2} (-1)^k * a[k]

Let me verify with sample:
n=3, a=[2,2,4]
R_1 = 4 + (-1)^0 * a[0] + (-1)^1 * a[1] = 4 + 1*2 + (-1)*2 = 4 + 2 - 2 = 4 ✓

Perfect!

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate R_1
r1 = a[n-1]  # A_N
for k in range(n - 1):
    if k % 2 == 0:
        r1 += a[k]
    else:
        r1 -= a[k]

# Calculate all R_i
r = [0] * n
r[0] = r1

for i in range(1, n):
    r[i] = 2 * a[i-1] - r[i-1]

print(*r)
```
